# Definition for a binary tree node.
# class TreeNode:
#     def __init__(self, val=0, left=None, right=None):
#         self.val = val
#         self.left = left
#         self.right = right
def tsum(root):
    if(root==None):
        return 0
    x= root.val+tsum(root.left)+tsum(root.right)
    return x
def fun(root,sm,mx):
    if(root==None):
        return 0
    a=fun(root.left,sm,mx)
    b=fun(root.right,sm,undefined_value)
    mx[0]=max(mx[0],a*(sm-a),b*(sm-b))
    return a+b+root.val
    
class Solution:
    def maxProduct(self, root: Optional[TreeNode]) -> int:
        mx=[0]
        sm=tsum(root)
        memo={}
        fun(root,sm,mx)
        return mx[0]%(10**9+7)

A practical pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.
- Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions.
- Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path.
- After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case.
- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.
- Once the failing path and its directly coupled region look consistent, finalize the code.

Let's craft fixed code:

```
from typing import Optional

# Definition for a binary tree node.
# class TreeNode:
#     def __init__(self, val=0, left=None, right=None):
#         self.val = val
#         self.left = left
#         self.right = right

def tsum(root):
    if root is None:
        return 0
    return root.val + tsum(root.left) + tsum(root.right)

def fun(root, sm, mx):
    if root is None:
        return 0
    a = fun(root.left, sm, mx)
    b = fun(root.right, sm, mx)
    mx[0] = max(mx[0], a * (sm - a), b * (sm - b))
    return a + b + root.val

class Solution:
    def maxProduct(self, root: Optional[TreeNode]) -> int:
        mx = [0]
        sm = tsum(root)
        fun(root, sm, mx)
        return mx[0] % (10**9 + 7)
```

```
class Solution:
    def maxProduct(self, root: TreeNode) -> int:
        self.s = 0
        def dfs(root):
            if not root: return 0
            self.s += root.val
            return root.val + dfs(root.left) + dfs(root.right)
        dfs(root)
        self.ans = 0
        def helper(root):
            if not root: return 0
            left = helper(root.left)
            right = helper(root.right)
            self.ans = max(self.ans, left*(self.s-left), right*(self.s-right))
            return left + right + root.val
        helper(root)
        return self.ans % (10**9+7)
```

from typing import Optional

# Definition for a binary tree node.
# class TreeNode:
#     def __init__(self, val=0, left=None, right=None):
#         self.val = val
#         self.left = left
#         self.right = right

def tsum(root):
    if root is None:
        return 0
    return root.val + tsum(root.left) + tsum(root.right)

def fun(root, sm, mx):
    if root is None:
        return 0
    a = fun(root.left, sm, mx)
    b = fun(root.right, sm, mx)
    mx[0] = max(mx[0], a * (sm - a), b * (sm - b))
    return a + b + root.val

class Solution:
    def maxProduct(self, root: Optional[TreeNode]) -> int:
        mx = [0]
        sm = tsum(root)
        fun(root, sm, mx)
        return mx[0] % (10**9 + 7)
```

```
class Solution:
    def maxProduct(self, root: TreeNode) -> int:
        self.total = 0
        def dfs(node):
            if not node: return 0
            self.total += node.val
            return dfs(node.left) + dfs(node.right) + node.val
        dfs(root)
        self.ans = 0
        def helper(node):
            if not node: return 0
            left = helper(node.left)
            right = helper(node.right)
            self.ans = max(self.ans, left*(self.total-left), right*(self.total-right))
            return left + right + node.val
        helper(root)
        return self.ans % (10**9+7)
```

Could implement iterative to avoid recursion entirely? Let's consider if we can write a robust iterative solution without too much complexity. We need compute subtree sums for each node and total sum. We can do postorder iterative using stack to compute subtree sums and track max. That would avoid recursion depth. But the prompt's code is recursive; changing to iterative is a larger fix but still correct. However, the user asked "fix the code" not "rewrite." Minimal fix preferred. Also iterative code might be longer and more error-prone. But we can do:

```
class Solution:
    def maxProduct(self, root: Optional[TreeNode]) -> int:
        total = 0
        stack = [(root, False)]
        sums = {}
        while stack:
            node, visited = stack.pop()
            if not node:
                continue
            if not visited:
                total += node.val
                stack.append((node, True))
                stack.append((node.left, False))
                stack.append((node.right, False))
            else:
                sums[node] = node.val + sums.get(node.left, 0) + sums.get(node.right, 0)
        ans = 0
        for node, s in sums.items():
            if node.left:
                ans = max(ans, sums[node.left] * (total - sums[node.left]))
            if node.right:
                ans = max(ans, sums[node.right] * (total - sums[node.right]))
        return ans % (10**9+7)
```
But this uses dictionary with TreeNode keys, okay. But it's a rewrite. The prompt likely expects fixing the original recursive code. We'll keep recursive.

The prompt's "repair-and-recheck" instruction: "Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency. Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions. Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path. After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case. The prompt's "repair-and-recheck" instruction: "Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local
</think>

=== RAW ANSWER ===
from typing import Optional

# Definition for a binary tree node.
# class TreeNode:
#     def __init__(self, val=0, left=None, right=None):
#         self.val = val
#         self.left = left
#         self.right = right

def tsum(root):
    if root is None:
        return 0
    return root.val + tsum(root.left) + tsum(root.right)

def fun(root, sm, mx):
    if root is None:
        return 0
    a = fun(root.left, sm, mx)
    b = fun(root.right, sm, mx)
    mx[0] = max(mx[0], a * (sm - a), b * (sm - b))
    return a + b + root.val

class Solution:
    def maxProduct(self, root: Optional[TreeNode]) -> int:
        mx = [0]
        sm = tsum(root)
        fun(root, sm, mx)
        return mx[0] % (10**9 + 7)
